- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Askia, leader of Songhai (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Songhai
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mandekalu Cavalry, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Sofa, Replacing Crossbowman (Requires technology: Machinery)
        - Unique Building: Tabya, Replacing Stone Works (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Unique Building: Gumey, Replacing Caravansary (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: Triple Gold from destroying Encampments and plundering Cities. Land Units gain the Amphibious and War Canoes Promotions.
      - Leader: Askia
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      
      ## Policies
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 8
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 3
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 4
        - Boldness: 6
        - WarBias: 7
        - HostileBias: 5
        - WarmongerHate: 1
        - NeutralBias: 3
        - FriendlyBias: 1
        - GuardedBias: 7
        - AfraidBias: 3
        - DiplomaticBalance: 5
        - Friendliness: 1
        - WorkWithWillingness: 7
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 8
        - Loyalty: 6
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 4
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 5
        - MinorCivWarBias: 4
        - DenounceWillingness: 6
        - Forgiveness: 4
        - Meanness: 5
        - Neediness: 5
        - Chattiness: 4
        - DeceptiveBias: 2
        - Rationale: Increased Air (85 from 85 maintained) and Ranged (85 from 80) to capitalize on Heavy Bomber production (Tombouctu, Kukia, Tondibi all building Heavy Bombers) and upcoming Stealth Bombers. Air power proving decisive against Japan (destroyed destroyer, dreadnought, aircraft). Diplomacy (80 from 75) prioritized for UN vote in 7 turns - must secure delegate lead vs Siam (20 vs 19). Defense/CityDefense at 85 remains appropriate given Zulu military supremacy (134K vs 9.6K). Nuke/UseNuke at 100/85 provides existential deterrence. Offensive posture sufficient (45) for Japan steamroll while consolidating gains around Kyoto/Tokyo against counterattacks.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Stealth
        - Rationale: Stealth Bombers (135 ranged strength) with first-strike capability are highest military priority. Zulus have Heavy Bombers and Mobile SAMs; Stealth provides decisive air superiority for both Japan war conclusion and Zulu containment. Current Heavy Bomber (event 211-212) in Jenne shows air power is effective. Advanced Ballistics completes in ~3 turns; maintaining Stealth research queue ensures we can counter Zulu air threat when it arrives (they have 68 techs vs our 67).
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Hero of the People (Policy)
        - Rationale: Order Level 3 Tenet (+25% Great Person rate, free Great Person) optimal timing. Free Great Person provides immediate strategic flexibility for tech acceleration via Great Scientist or military via Great General. Great Engineer also generated in Gao (event 205-206). With UN vote in 7 turns, accelerating Great Scientist helps close tech gap vs Zulus (68 vs 67 techs) and unlock spaceship components if diplomacy fails. Hero of the People synergizes with current GreatPeople flavor (70).
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Brazil capitulated this turn and is now our vassal. Resetting relationship modifier to 0 since vassal status overrides normal diplomatic calculations. Brazil contributes resources, gold per turn (negative will be managed), and provides vote support for diplomatic victory considerations. Their single remaining city (Rio de Janeiro) is effectively under our control.
        - UpdatedTurn: 193
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Netherlands relationship remains 0/0. Netherlands is effectively defeated - only 1 city remaining (Arnhem) with Breda just captured by Songhai. Maximum hostility served no purpose previously. Netherlands will likely capitulate soon or be eliminated. Strategic focus should remain on primary threats: Zulus (4 capitals, 50% Domination) and Japan (6625 military strength). No need to expend diplomatic resources on a defeated secondary enemy.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### The Zulus
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -60
        - Rationale: Maximum hostility following Zulu war declaration (turn 404 events 141-145). Zulus now the primary existential threat with 18783 military vs my 10089, 5 capitals (62% Domination). Peace treaty expired and they chose aggression - must reflect total war posture despite shared Order ideology.
        - UpdatedTurn: 405
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -60
        - Rationale: Japan hostility maintained at maximum (-60/-60). Despite Japan capitulation events and trade, Japan remains active opponent with 10,878 military, declared war, ideologically incompatible (Autocracy vs Order), and our Japan front war score improved to 56% favorable with steamroll posture near Kyoto. Maximum hostility appropriate for dangerous secondary opponent continuing to resist our advances.
        - UpdatedTurn: 407
        
        ### Korea
        - Public: -10
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: Korea remains Order-aligned vassal of Zulus (-10/-10 unchanged). Despite shared ideology, Korea is actively opposing war efforts. Zone 1057 shows Korean units coordinating activities; they remain Zulu proxy force. Not worth diplomatic capital to improve relationship while Korea supports primary threat. Low negative acknowledges weak military (1740 strength, reduced from 1800+) but maintains war pressure needed to contain Zulu influence. Focus remains on Japan capitulation and Zulu containment.
        - UpdatedTurn: 423
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: -10
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: Siam relationship maintained at -10/-10. Despite shared Order ideology and relatively weak military (714 strength), Siam is Zulu proxy with 2 cities remaining. Siam proposes United Nations (could enable diplomatic victory for me if elected). Low hostility acknowledges Siam's weakness (only 0.2x my military) while maintaining war posture. Siam provides 19 delegates - capturing Siam could reduce competing votes vs Zulus for world leader, but Zulu threat (134K military) requires prioritization over secondary conflicts.
        - UpdatedTurn: 426
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: UnitedNations grand strategy remains optimal. Diplomatic victory achievable in 7 turns with current delegate lead (20 vs Siam 19). Gold (1318 at 22 GPT) and city-state alliances (Dublin, Palatium, Antananarivo, Lutetia, Harappa) maintain diplomatic influence. Science (2971/turn) and Stealth research provide military security against Zulus. Japan war progressing favorably (zone 19051 favorable 3:1 strength, captured Kyoto/Tokyo). Strategic Defense Systems completing across major cities strengthen air defense. No strategic changes required - focus is diplomatic vote preparation and military technology advancement.
        - GrandStrategy: UnitedNations
        
        ### Flavors
        - Offense: 45
        - Defense: 85
        - Mobilization: 55
        - CityDefense: 85
        - MilitaryTraining: 75
        - Recon: 30
        - Ranged: 80

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 41
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 6 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Songhai Sphere of Influence: Palatium:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Siamese Sphere of Influence: Jakarta:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Siamese Sphere of Influence: Ecbatana:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Antananarivo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Buddhism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Sanction: Songhai:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - Proposer: Japan
          - Enact: United Nations:
            - Description: Begins the United Nations project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : United Nations wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World. Also, when you expend a Great Person, earn 25 Influence with all City-States you have met. Triggers a Golden Age, as well as the United Nations Special Session of the World Congress if the world has reached the Atomic Era.)
              : Receive an immediate boost of 25 Influence with all City-States. The Consulate National Wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.)
              : Receive a free Great Diplomat.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Siam
          - Repeal: Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - Proposer: The Zulus

  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Askia, leader of Songhai (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Songhai
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mandekalu Cavalry, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Sofa, Replacing Crossbowman (Requires technology: Machinery)
        - Unique Building: Tabya, Replacing Stone Works (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Unique Building: Gumey, Replacing Caravansary (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: Triple Gold from destroying Encampments and plundering Cities. Land Units gain the Amphibious and War Canoes Promotions.
      - Leader: Askia
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the world.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      
      ## Policies
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-persona
      - args:
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 6
        - Rationale: Adjusting persona to prioritize peaceful resolution with non-threatening, ideologically aligned civs like Siam (raising DiplomaticBalance and Friendliness) and increasing competitiveness/friendliness with city-states (MinorCivCompetitiveness/MinorCivFriendlyBias) to prepare for the upcoming UN vote and protect our CS allies.
        - DiplomaticBalance: 7
        - Friendliness: 3
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 6
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Rationale: Encourage our staff to seek and accept peace with Siam. Siam is extremely weak (714 military), shares our Order ideology, is at war with our main enemy the Zulus, and is the proposer of the UN. Peace with Siam reduces our active war fronts and secures a key diplomatic partner against the Zulus.
        - Private: 40
        - Public: 40
        - TargetID: 5
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Stealth
        - Rationale: Stealth Bombers are our highest military priority. They provide ultimate air superiority, allowing us to conclude the Japan war quickly and defend our western enclave against the Zulu death stacks. Advanced Ballistics completes in 2 turns, so keeping Stealth next is crucial.
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Order L1 tenet provides +25% Great Person rate and a free Great Person of choice. This allows us to get a Great Scientist to rush Stealth technology or a Great Engineer to rush the UN wonder once the project is enacted.
        - Policy: Hero of the People
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: UnitedNations
        - Flavors:
          - CityDefense: 85
          - Production: 80
          - Gold: 90
          - Science: 90
          - Defense: 85
          - Diplomacy: 85
          - Air: 90
          - UseNuke: 85
          - Nuke: 100
      - rationale: Maintaining UnitedNations grand strategy. Prioritizing Defense and CityDefense to withstand the Zulu push in the west, while keeping Air and Nuke high to capitalize on our Bomber fleet and A-bombs. Increasing Diplomacy, Production, and Science to ensure we win the UN project and unlock Stealth Bombers rapidly.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Current Situation**
            
            My current focus is on assessing our geopolitical landscape. We're engaged in conflict with Korea, currently a vassal of the Zulus. Japan, under Oda Nobunaga, remains a factor, operating under an Autocracy.
            
            **Evaluating Diplomatic Entanglements**
            
            I'm currently dissecting our complex web of international relations. We're in active conflict with Korea and the Zulus, with Siam also at war due to alliances, despite their "Friendly" approach. Japan, under Nobunaga's Autocracy, is a persistent player.
            
            **Considering Policy Options**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our current "Hero of the People" policy given the upcoming policy vote. It seems highly beneficial for boosting Great Person generation, which could significantly impact our science and diplomatic efforts. I'm weighing this against other potential policy choices.
            
            **Reviewing Diplomatic Victory Status**
            
            The United Nations vote is approaching, with Siam proposing it. I'm assessing delegate counts and our city-state alliances to determine our voting strength and potential impact on the outcome. Siam has also proposed sanctions.
            
            **Securing Diplomatic Victory**
            
            My focus is now on securing a diplomatic victory. The UN vote looms, and with Songhai's delegates, I believe we're close. I need to eliminate Siam or secure more city-states to guarantee success. Our declining GPT is a concern.
            
            **Prioritizing Defensive Measures**
            
            I'm shifting focus to immediate defense. Multiple cities are building critical infrastructure like the Strategic Defense System and Atomic Bombs. We also have new bombers and destroyers in training, and importantly, I've noticed we are in active combat.
            
            **Defending Vulnerable Allies**
            
            I'm actively monitoring the Zulu assault on our ally Dublin, rerouting forces to its defense. Simultaneously, I'm pushing our superior Bazooka units from Kyoto towards Osaka, aiming to overwhelm Japan's defenses there and secure our position.
            
            **Assessing Battlefield Readiness**
            
            I am analyzing troop and unit deployments across our territories and adjacent enemy zones, noting troop compositions for both our forces and Japan's. This includes evaluating the dominance of our forces in key areas and identifying immediate threats and opportunities.
            
            **Analyzing Troop Deployments**
            
            I'm currently assessing our troop and unit positions, particularly focusing on the dense concentration of Bazookas surrounding Haarlem and Wittenberg. This close proximity to the enemy, especially with Japan's forces near Hakodate, necessitates a review of our defensive posture and potential offensive thrusts.
            
            **Recognizing Dire Threat**
            
            I've just realized the Zulus possess an overwhelming military force of 134K, dwarfing our 9.6K. They are actively pushing into Dublin, a critical city, from their occupied territory of Huari. Our current troop concentrations, while locally strong, are insufficient against this global imbalance.
            
            **Prioritizing Immediate Threats**
            
            I'm urgently reassessing our precarious position. The Zulu military vastly outnumbers us, and they're advancing on Dublin. Despite our atomic bomb production and research, survival for the next 5-9 turns is critical, especially with a World Congress vote and the UN proposal looming.
            
            **Confirming Global Hostilities**
            
            I've confirmed we're embroiled in a massive global conflict. We are actively at war with Siam, Korea, Japan, and the Zulus, with significant war weariness accumulating across all fronts. This widespread hostility necessitates an immediate review of our defensive and offensive strategies.
            
            **Reassessing Grand Strategy**
            
            My focus has shifted dramatically. The looming Zulu threat is paramount, their military might is overwhelming. I'm re-evaluating our entire grand strategy away from diplomatic victory and towards immediate survival, considering how to best defend our western enclaves.
            
            **Analyzing Global War Status**
            
            I'm realizing the extent of our global conflict; we are at war with everyone and facing significant war weariness. The Zulu military significantly outnumbers us, posing an immediate existential threat, especially with their capital control and advancement. My focus has completely shifted from diplomatic victory to urgent survival.
            
            **Recognizing Existential Threat**
            
            I've just grasped the critical reality: the Zulus control five of the game's eight capitals, and we're at war. If they capture any of our three remaining capitals, they will achieve a domination victory. Survival is now the absolute priority.
            
            **Assessing Zulu Advance**
            
            I'm analyzing the immediate Zulu threat. While they control five capitals and possess overwhelming military might, their current troop concentrations appear focused on Dublin, not directly on my remaining capitals. I'm assessing the proximity and strength of their forces near my core territories.
            
            **Locating Primary Threat**
            
            I've pinpointed the Zulu's main military strength. It appears their massive army isn't immediately threatening our core cities but is positioned near our western enclave, specifically around Haarlem and The Hague. Their vast empire stretches across the western side of the map, directly adjacent to our vulnerable frontier.
            
            **Analyzing Zulu Positioning**
            
            My focus is on the Zulu military's placement. Their massive force appears concentrated near our western enclave, specifically around Haarlem and The Hague, rather than directly threatening our capitals. Their vast empire directly borders our vulnerable frontier.
            
            **Shifting Grand Strategy**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our primary victory condition. While diplomatic was the aim, the overwhelming Zulu threat and our close proximity in technology suggests a Science Victory is now a more viable and safer path to survival. I'm considering accelerating spaceship part production.
            
            **Considering Offensive Push**
            
            I am reconsidering our offensive posture. While the Zulu threat is immense, Japan's 13,000 military strength presents an opportunity. Capturing Osaka, their key city, could significantly weaken them and potentially lead to their capitulation or vassalization, freeing up resources.
            
            **Launching Offensive Campaign**
            
            My current focus is on exploiting a strategic window against Japan. With a formidable air force and concentrated Bazooka units near Kyoto, I'm preparing to assault Osaka. This offensive aims to cripple their war effort and potentially force their capitulation.
            
            **Developing Nuclear Strategy**
            
            I am now focusing on our nuclear capabilities. With four Atomic Bombs under construction, I see their potential to disrupt Zulu advances near Haarlem or Wittenberg. Our existing uranium supply of 7 further supports this strategy, ensuring we can field these deterrents.
            
            **Leveraging Production Power**
            
            I'm realizing our immense production capabilities can secure the United Nations wonder. This strategically aligns with our current 'United Nations' grand strategy, promising a significant boost in delegates and city-state influence, potentially solidifying our path to victory.
            
            **Adjusting Flavor Priorities**
            
            I am now adjusting our strategic flavors. Given the overwhelming Zulu threat and our reliance on atomic weapons and air power, I am prioritizing `Defense`, `CityDefense`, `Nuke`, `UseNuke`, and `Air` to ensure survival and leverage our offensive capabilities.
            
            **Adjusting Research Focus**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our current research path, shifting from Advanced Ballistics to prioritize Stealth. This move aligns with our enhanced focus on defense and leveraging advanced capabilities, ensuring we stay ahead of potential threats and capitalize on emerging technologies.
            
            **Selecting Next Research**
            
            I've confirmed Stealth is available and the highest priority for research. This will unlock critical air superiority with Stealth Bombers against both Japan and the Zulus. I need to reinforce this decision using `set-research`.
            
            **Evaluating Policy Impact**
            
            I'm analyzing the strategic implications of selecting Communism versus Hero of the People. Communism's production bonus for Wonders could expedite UN completion, while Hero of the People boosts Great Person generation, potentially speeding up Stealth research or UN construction with a Great Engineer.
            
            **Evaluating Policy Synergy**
            
            I'm considering how a Great Scientist could accelerate Stealth research, making "Hero of the People" more appealing than Communism for its Wonder production, especially since Great Engineers can rush the UN. This could significantly boost our scientific and diplomatic advantages.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Shifts**
            
            I'm reassessing our diplomatic stance with Siam, given they proposed the UN. Our current war, despite their distance and limited cities, seems to drain resources unnecessarily. Making peace could secure our western flank and potentially open avenues for future negotiations.
            
            **Seeking Peace with Siam**
            
            I'm reassessing our conflict with Siam. Given their weakened state, current wars, and shared enemy in the Zulus, pursuing peace now seems strategically advantageous. This could free up resources and potentially create a valuable ally against a common foe.
            
            **Initiating Peace Talks**
            
            I'm focusing on establishing a peace treaty with Siam. Their current military weakness, ongoing conflict with the Zulus, and their role as the UN proposer make them an ideal target for de-escalation. This will allow us to better concentrate on the Zulu and Japanese threats.
            
            **Adjusting Relationship Values**
            
            I am now focusing on optimizing our relationships for peace and war. I'm increasing our diplomatic standing with Siam to encourage a peace treaty, while maintaining hostile stances towards Japan and the Zulus. I'm also reviewing our `set-persona` values for optimal gameplay.
            
            **Adjusting AI Personality**
            
            I am now adjusting our AI personality values. Increasing Diplomatic Balance to 7 and Friendliness to 3 should help facilitate peace with Siam and improve diplomatic interactions, while maintaining Boldness at 6 and WarBias at 7 to manage the ongoing conflict with Japan.
            
            **Boosting City-State Influence**
            
            I'm now focusing on securing our city-state alliances to bolster our UN victory path. I'm increasing both Minor Civ Competitiveness and Friendliness to encourage stronger relationships and influence over these crucial entities, ensuring we can leverage their delegates effectively.
            
            **Securing Peace with Siam**
            
            I'm actively pursuing peace with Siam. Their weakened state, shared ideology, and war with the Zulus make them a prime candidate for de-escalation. This move aims to simplify our conflict landscape and potentially foster an alliance against our primary threat.
            
            **Finalizing Persona Adjustments**
            
            I'm finalizing adjustments to our AI persona. My focus is on ensuring successful peace talks with Siam by increasing diplomatic balance and friendliness, while simultaneously bolstering our city-state influence to secure the UN vote.

        - #2:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: 41gTdObsOaEw7PTC
          - toolName: set-relationship
          - input:
            - Rationale: Encourage our staff to seek and accept peace with Siam. Siam is extremely weak (714 military), shares our Order ideology, is at war with our main enemy the Zulus, and is the proposer of the UN. Peace with Siam reduces our active war fronts and secures a key diplomatic partner against the Zulus.
            - Private: 40
            - Public: 40
            - TargetID: 5
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #3:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: 3bTDbT7VyrZVUsVQ
          - toolName: set-research
          - input:
            - Technology: Stealth
            - Rationale: Stealth Bombers are our highest military priority. They provide ultimate air superiority, allowing us to conclude the Japan war quickly and defend our western enclave against the Zulu death stacks. Advanced Ballistics completes in 2 turns, so keeping Stealth next is crucial.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #4:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: C2811BY9xNFgeghX
          - toolName: set-policy
          - input:
            - Rationale: Order L1 tenet provides +25% Great Person rate and a free Great Person of choice. This allows us to get a Great Scientist to rush Stealth technology or a Great Engineer to rush the UN wonder once the project is enacted.
            - Policy: Hero of the People
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #5:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: TSRcH1XFFQx54Goq
          - toolName: set-flavors
          - input:
            - Rationale: Maintaining UnitedNations grand strategy. Prioritizing Defense and CityDefense to withstand the Zulu push in the west, while keeping Air and Nuke high to capitalize on our Bomber fleet and A-bombs. Increasing Diplomacy, Production, and Science to ensure we win the UN project and unlock Stealth Bombers rapidly.
            - GrandStrategy: UnitedNations
            - Flavors:
              - CityDefense: 85
              - Production: 80
              - Gold: 90
              - Science: 90
              - Defense: 85
              - Diplomacy: 85
              - Air: 90
              - UseNuke: 85
              - Nuke: 100
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
